[Methodology](/2024/methodology/)\n\n# 2 Technology\n\nEach year we explore the tools and technologies developers are currently using and the ones they want to use.\n\nThis year, we included new questions about embedded technology tools and industry-sourced, community-vetted technology options.\n\n* [2.1. (12.2%)\n\n### [Databases](#1-databases)\n\nPostgreSQL debuted in the developer survey in 2018 when 33% of developers reported using it, compared with the most popular option that year: MySQL, in use by 59% of developers.       "title": "2024 Stack Overflow Developer Survey",
      "description": "Rust continues to be the most-admired programming language with an 83% score this year. Company info Company size 4.3.",
      "url": "https://survey.stackoverflow.co/2024/",
      "content": "Stack Overflow Developer Survey\n\nProducts\n\n1. [Stack Overflow for Teams Where developers & technologists share private knowledge with\n   coworkers](https://stackoverflow.co/teams/?utm_medium=referral&utm_source=survey&utm_campaign=dev-survey-2024&utm_content=teams-dropdown)\n2. [Advertising & Talent Reach devs & technologists worldwide about your product,\n   service or employer brand](https://stackoverflow.co/advertising/?utm_medium=referral&utm_source=survey&utm_campaign=dev-survey-2024&utm_content=advertising-dropdown)\n3. [OverflowAI GenAI features for Teams](https://stackoverflow.co/teams/ai/?utm_medium=referral&utm_source=survey&utm_campaign=dev-survey-2024&utm_content=overflowai-dropdown)\n4. [OverflowAPI Train & fine-tune LLMs](https://stackoverflow.co/api-solutions/?utm_medium=referral&utm_source=survey&utm_campaign=dev-survey-2024&utm_content=overflowapi-dropdown)\n5. [Labs The future of collective knowledge sharing](https://stackoverflow.co/labs/?utm_medium=referral&utm_source=survey&utm_campaign=dev-survey-2024&utm_content=labs-dropdown)\n6. [About the company](https://stackoverflow.co/?utm_medium=referral&utm_source=survey&utm_campaign=dev-survey-2024&utm_content=company-dropdown) [Visit the blog](https://stackoverflow.blog/?utm_medium=referral&utm_source=survey&utm_campaign=dev-survey-2024&utm_content=blog-dropdown)\n\nIn May 2024, over 65,000 developers responded to our annual survey about coding, the technologies and tools they use and want to learn, AI, and developer experience at work. Check out the results and see what's new for Stack Overflow users.\n\n![](/2024/img/hero.png)\n\n1. [Developer Profile](/2024/developer-profile/)\n2. [Technology](/2024/technology/)\n3. [AI](/2024/ai/)\n4. [Work](/2024/work/)\n5. [Community](/2024/community/)\n6. [Professional Developers](/2024/professional-developers/)\n7. [Methodology](/2024/methodology/)\n\n[Methodology](/2024/methodology/)\n\n65,437\n\nCoders from 185 countries around the world.\n\n[Technology](/2024/technology/)\n\n62.3%\n\nHave used JavaScript in the past year.\n\n[Methodology](/2024/methodology/)\n\n48,019\n\nRespondants shared salary information.\n\n[Developer profile](/2024/developer-profile/)\n→\n[Key territories](/2024/developer-profile#key-territories)\n\n## Top 10 countries\n\nUnknow graph No description  🇺🇸 USA18.9%🇩🇪 Germany8.4%🇮🇳 India7.2%🇬🇧 UK5.5%🇺🇦 Ukraine4.6%🇨🇦 Canada3.6%🇫🇷 France3.6%🇵🇱 Poland2.6%🇳🇱 Netherlands2.5%🇧🇷 Brazil2.3%\n\n[Community](/2024/community/)\n\n76.3%\n\nHave a Stack Overflow account.\n\n[Advertising & Talent\n\nReach devs & technologists worldwide about your product, service\nor employer brand.](https://stackoverflow.co/advertising/?utm_medium=referral&utm_source=survey&utm_campaign=dev-survey-2024&utm_content=adveetising-dropdown)\n\n[**Now available!** A suite of GenAI tools for Stack Overflow\nfor Teams that helps connect employees to knowledge faster.](https://stackoverflow.co/teams/ai/?utm_medium=referral&utm_source=survey&utm_campaign=dev-survey-2024&utm_content=overflowai-dropdown)\n\n[Developer Profile](/2024/developer-profile/) → [Education](/2024/developer-profile#education)\n\n## Educational attainment\n\n66% of developers have a BA/BS or MA/MS degree despite only 49% of developers learning to code at school.\n\n[Educational attainment →](/2024/developer-profile#1-educational-attainment)\n\n[Developer Profile](/2024/developer-profile/) → [Learning to code](/2024/developer-profile#learning-to-code)\n\n## Learning to code\n\nOnline resources are the top choice for developers to learn code according to 82% of responses.\n\nRespondents 18 - 24 years old are most likely to learn code at traditional schools of all the age groups, and consistently so for the last three years.\n\n[Learning to code →](/2024/developer-profile#2-learning-to-code)\n\n[Developer Profile](/2024/developer-profile/) → [Learning to code](/2024/developer-profile#learning-to-code)\n\n## Technical documentation preferences to learn how to code\n\nAPI and SDK documents are the documentation source of choice for 90% of developers.\n\n[Technical documentation preferences to learn how to code →](/2024/developer-profile#2-technical-documentation-preferences-to-learn-how-to-code)\n\n[Technology](/2024/technology/) → [Most popular technologies](/2024/technology#most-popular-technologies)\n\n## Databases\n\nPostgreSQL debuted in the developer survey in 2018 when 33% of developers reported using it, compared with the most popular option that year: MySQL, in use by 59% of developers. Six years later, PostgreSQL is used by 49% of developers and is the most popular database for the second year in a row.\n\n[Databases →](/2024/technology#1-databases)\n\n[Technology](/2024/technology/) → [Most popular technologies](/2024/technology#most-popular-technologies)\n\n## Other tools\n\nDocker is used the most by professional developers (59%) and npm is used the most by developers learning to code (45%).\n\n[Other tools →](/2024/technology#1-other-tools)\n\n[Technology](/2024/technology/) → [Most popular technologies](/2024/technology#most-popular-technologies)\n\n## Asynchronous tools\n\nJira and Confluence top the list for most used asynchronous tools developers use for the third year.\n\n[Asynchronous tools →](/2024/technology#1-asynchronous-tools)\n\n[Technology](/2024/technology/) → [Admired and Desired](/2024/technology#admired-and-desired)\n\n## Programming, scripting, and markup languages\n\nJavaScript, Python and SQL are all highly-desired and admired programming languages, but Rust continues to be the most-admired programming language with an 83% score this year.\n\n[Programming, scripting, and markup languages →](/2024/technology#2-programming-scripting-and-markup-languages)\n\n[Technology](/2024/technology/) → [Admired and Desired](/2024/technology#admired-and-desired)\n\n## Web frameworks and technologies\n\n73% of developers that used it want to keep working with Svelte. Fun fact: Our team at Stack Overflow used Svelte for the first time in building our 2024 Developer Survey results site. We could go on and on about Svelte, [listen to us do just that in a interview with one of our own](https://stackoverflow.blog/2023/10/31/why-stack-overflow-is-embracing-svelte/).\n\n[Web frameworks and technologies →](/2024/technology#2-web-frameworks-and-technologies)\n\n[Technology](/2024/technology/) → [Admired and Desired](/2024/technology#admired-and-desired)\n\n## AI Search and Developer Tools\n\nMost developers who use ChatGPT want to keep using it (75% Admired), while most who use Replit Ghostwrite or Tabnine are least likely to keep using it (36% Admired). If you aren't familiar with Replit, [we have an interview for you to familiarize yourself](https://stackoverflow.blog/2023/09/14/multiplayer-programming-on-mobile-a-chat-with-replit-ceo-amjad-masad/).\n\n[AI Search and Developer Tools →](/2024/technology#2-ai-search-and-developer-tools)\n\n[Technology](/2024/technology/) → [Worked with vs. want to work with](/2024/technology#worked-with-vs-want-to-work-with)\n\n## AI Search and Developer Tools\n\nMost developers use ChatGPT of all the AI tools, and 74% want to keep using it next year. 41% of ChatGPT users want to use GitHub Copilot next year.\n\n[AI Search and Developer Tools →](/2024/technology#3-ai-search-and-developer-tools)\n\n[Technology](/2024/technology/) → [Top paying technologies](/2024/technology#top-paying-technologies)\n\n## Top paying technologies\n\nErlang developers take the top spot this year for highest reported median salary.\n\n[Top paying technologies →](/2024/technology#4-top-paying-technologies)\n\n[AI](/2024/ai/) → [Sentiment and usage](/2024/ai#sentiment-and-usage)\n\n## AI tools in the development process\n\n76% of all respondents are using or are planning to use AI tools in their development process this year, an increase from last year (70%). Many more developers are currently using AI tools this year, too (62% vs. 44%).\n\n[AI tools in the development process →](/2024/ai#1-ai-tools-in-the-development-process)\n\n[AI](/2024/ai/) → [Developer tools](/2024/ai#developer-tools)\n\n## AI tools next year\n\nIn the next year, most developers agree that AI tools will be more integrated mostly in the ways they are documenting code (81%), testing code (80%), and writing code (76%).\n\n[AI tools next year →](/2024/ai#2-ai-tools-next-year)\n\n[AI](/2024/ai/) → [Efficacy and Ethics](/2024/ai#efficacy-and-ethics)\n\n## Are AI tools a threat to your job\n\n70% of professional developers do not perceive AI as a threat to their job.\n\n[Are AI tools a threat to your job →](/2024/ai#3-are-ai-tools-a-threat-to-your-job)\n\n[AI](/2024/ai/) → [Efficacy and Ethics](/2024/ai#efficacy-and-ethics)\n\n## Most important ethical issues for AI\n\nMisinformation and disinformation in AI results are a top ethical concern in this year's survey for 79% of developers. Source attribution is the next top ethical concern (65%) which [Stack Overflow is working to protect for our community members](https://stackoverflow.blog/2024/02/29/defining-socially-responsible-ai-how-we-select-api-partners/).\n\n[Most important ethical issues for AI →](/2024/ai#3-most-important-ethical-issues-for-ai)\n\n[Work](/2024/work/) → [Employment](/2024/work#employment)\n\n## Employment status\n\n84% of respondents are working: either part-time, freelancing, or full-time.\n\n[Employment status →](/2024/work#1-employment-status)\n\n[Work](/2024/work/) → [Employment](/2024/work#employment)\n\n## Work environment\n\nThe number of hybrid developers has stayed consistent this year with last year (42%), while the percentage of developers working in-person has increased (20%) for the third year in a row compared to 15% in 2022 and 16% in 2023.\n\n[Work environment →](/2024/work#1-work-environment)\n\n[Work](/2024/work/) → [Purchasing technology](/2024/work#purchasing-technology)\n\n## Tech endorsement factors\n\nAPIs are pretty cool, but did you know that 75% of developers are more likely to endorse your technology if you provide access to APIs? Make sure to let back-end developers and engineering managers know about the API integrations you offer: they are even more likely to endorse your tech than the average coder.\n\n[Tech endorsement factors →](/2024/work#4-tech-endorsement-factors)\n\n[Community](/2024/community/) → [Stack Overflow site use](/2024/community#stack-overflow-site-use)\n\n## Frequency of visiting Stack Overflow\n\n93% of respondents visit Stack Overflow at least multiple times per month if not multiple times per day.\n\n[Frequency of visiting Stack Overflow →](/2024/community#1-frequency-of-visiting-stack-overflow)\n\n[Community](/2024/community/) → [Participation and Feedback](/2024/community#participation-and-feedback)\n\n## How you choose to use Stack Overflow\n\nEveryone agrees that finding answers quickly is the primary reason to use Stack Overflow. Understandably, those learning to code are more likely to use Stack Overflow to learn coding skills than professional developers or other coders.\n\n[How you choose to use Stack Overflow →](/2024/community#2-how-you-choose-to-use-stack-overflow)\n\n[Professional Developers](/2024/professional-developers/) → [Productivity Impacts](/2024/professional-developers#productivity-impacts)\n\n## Years of professional work experience\n\n1 in 4 are 1 to 4: as in 25% of professional developers report having one to four years of work experience. The majority of respondents are early-to-mid career professionals (nine or less years experience) and the majority of people managers are mid-to-later career professionals (10+ years experience).\n\n[Years of professional work experience →](/2024/professional-developers#1-years-of-professional-work-experience)\n\n[Professional Developers](/2024/professional-developers/) → [Productivity Impacts](/2024/professional-developers#productivity-impacts)\n\n## Daily time spent searching for answers/solutions\n\n61% of all respondents spend more than 30 minutes a day searching for answers or solutions to problems.\n\nPeople managers spend less time searching than individual contributors (40% vs. 36% spend 30 minutes or less).\n\n[Daily time spent searching for answers/solutions →](/2024/professional-developers#1-daily-time-spent-searching-for-answers-solutions)\n\n# Survey index\n\n1. [1.2. Learning to code](/2024/developer-profile#learning-to-code)\n      1. [Learning to code](/2024/developer-profile#2-learning-to-code)\n      2. Bring the best of human thought and AI automation together at your work.\n   [Learn more](https://stackoverflow.co/internal/?utm_medium=referral&utm_source=stackoverflowmeta-community&utm_campaign=side-bar&utm_content=explore-teams-compact)\n\n**Stack Internal**\n\nKnowledge at work\n\nBring the best of human thought and AI automation together at your work.\n\n[Explore Stack Internal](https://stackoverflow.co/internal/?utm_medium=referral&utm_source=stackoverflowmeta-community&utm_campaign=side-bar&utm_content=explore-teams-compact-popover)\n\n# [The 2024 Developer Survey](/questions/430298/the-2024-developer-survey)\n\n[Ask Question](/questions/ask)\n\nAsked\n2 years ago\n\nModified\n[10 months ago](?lastactivity \"2025-08-05 08:47:25Z\")\n\nViewed\n46k times\n\nThis question shows research effort; it is useful and clear\n\n56\n\nThis question does not show any research effort; it is unclear or not useful\n\nSave this question.\n\nShow activity on this post.\n\n#### July 24, 2024 Update: The 2024 Dev Survey results are now [live](https://survey.stackoverflow.co/2024/?utm_medium=referral&utm_source=metaexchange-community&utm_campaign=dev-survey-2024&utm_content=meta-post-results)! We want to thank everyone again for their participation and cannot wait to see you next year.\n\n---\n\nUpdate: Due to the holiday and other factors, we have decided to extend the deadline to June 20th to provide more time for responses.\n\n---\n\nThe 14th annual Developer Survey is open to the public today. 13 years ago Stack Overflow put out the first iteration of this survey. It had 19 questions, received ~2,500 responses, and SQL was the most popular programming language. Now with a few more questions, including a new section on embedded technology, we invite you to weigh in. The survey will remain open until June 20th, 2024.\n\n[Take the 2024 Developer Survey here](https://stackoverflow.az1.qualtrics.com/jfe/form/SV_6rJVT6XXsfTo1JI?utm_medium=referral&utm_source=metaexchange-community&utm_campaign=dev-survey-2024&utm_content=meta-post).\n\nSurvey results will remain publicly available under the Open Database License. You can find past year’s survey datasets on [our insights page](https://survey.stackoverflow.co/) as well as on [Kaggle](https://www.kaggle.com/organizations/stackoverflow/datasets). We’ll update both with the latest data once the survey results are available.\n\nIf you use security or ad-blocking plugins, you may see some error messages. Our third-party survey provider, Qualtrics, does not work well with certain ad blockers and security software. To avoid error messages that prevent you from taking the survey, please try specifically unblocking Qualtrics in your plugin or pausing the plugin while you take the survey. Additionally, as a reminder, Qualtrics blocks certain countries from accessing their site and data. The list of blocked countries is: Cuba, Iran, North Korea, Syria, and the Crimea region of Ukraine (including Sevastopol). Finally, some users in China may have issues due to restrictions imposed by local internet service providers.\n\nNote that, as with last year's survey, we will be asking a smaller quantity of personal demographic questions. If you discover any bugs in the survey or have any questions or concerns, please feel free to add them below this post as answers.\n\n* [discussion](/questions/tagged/discussion \"show questions tagged 'discussion'\")\n* [survey](/questions/tagged/survey \"show questions tagged 'survey'\")\n* [survey-2024](/questions/tagged/survey-2024 \"show questions tagged 'survey-2024'\")\n\n[Share](/q/430298)\n\nShare a link to this question\n\nCopy link[CC BY-SA 4.0](https://creativecommons.org/licenses/by-sa/4.0/ \"The current license for this post: CC BY-SA 4.0\")\n\nShort permalink to this question\n\nFollow\n\nFollow this question to receive notifications\n\n[edited Jul 24, 2024 at 14:03](/posts/430298/revisions \"show all edits to this post\")\n\n[![philipxy's user avatar](https://www.gravatar.com/avatar/ec24a4fbe284c5379ba108a58a13fe8f?s=64&d=identicon&r=PG&f=y&so-version=2)](/users/3404097/philipxy)\n\n[philipxy](/users/3404097/philipxy)\n\n15.2k44 gold badges2121 silver badges2424 bronze badges\n\nasked May 20, 2024 at 13:51\n\n[![Erin Asks's user avatar](https://i.sstatic.net/DrA8L.png?s=64)](/users/20111849/erin-asks)\n\n[Erin Asks](/users/20111849/erin-asks)Staff\n\n5122 gold badges99 silver badges1414 bronze badges\n\n8\n\n* 28\n\n  *we will be asking a smaller quantity of personal demographic questions* Hurray! I took the survey and was pleasantly surprised to see that the only personal questions (perhaps more in the optional part I didn't complete) were age, educational level and country - all of which are, IMHO, quite reasonable and relevant for a large company trying to figure who (and where) their users are.\n\n  manassehkatz-Moving 2 Codidact\n\n  –\n  [manassehkatz-Moving 2 Codidact](/users/2475129/manassehkatz-moving-2-codidact \"3,064 reputation\")\n\n  2024-05-20 15:09:08 +00:00\n\n  [Commented\n  May 20, 2024 at 15:09](#comment997446_430298)\n* 16\n\n  What exactly is the incentive for me to take the web programming survey? You are asking me to spend my free time answering question that are completely irrelevant to my branch of programming and consequently I'm not interested in learning the results from the survey either. Why would I take this survey?\n\n  Lundin\n\n  –\n  [Lundin](/users/584518/lundin \"221,836 reputation\")\n\n  2024-05-21 06:54:40 +00:00\n\n  [Commented\n  May 21, 2024 at 6:54](#comment997503_430298)\n* 21\n\n  Cool that its out, I no longer participate in any developer survey from SO/SE just simply due to the direction this entire company has gone.\n\n  JonH\n\n  –\n  [JonH](/users/168703/jonh \"33,247 reputation\")\n\n  2024-05-21 19:23:29 +00:00\n\n  [Commented\n  May 21, 2024 at 19:23](#comment997632_430298)\n* 2\n\n  Nice to see HTMX on the list this time :) Especially since there are relatively little questions to the SO tag (which says more about how easy it is to learn and apply that tech rather than how popular it is).\n\n  Gimby\n\n  –\n  [Gimby](/users/424903/gimby \"5,399 reputation\")\n\n  2024-05-24 07:54:47 +00:00\n\n  [Commented\n  May 24, 2024 at 7:54](#comment997866_430298)\n* 1\n\n  Ukraine stats are obviously fake. See real numbers in 2021. The Survey became a joke.\n\n  Nakilon\n\n  –\n  [Nakilon](/users/322020/nakilon \"35,217 reputation\")\n\n  2024-07-28 03:39:28 +00:00\n\n  [Commented\n  Jul 28, 2024 at 3:39](#comment1003421_430298)\n* @manassehkatz-Moving2Codidact Why do you feel asking age is reasonable and relevant for a large company? That seems like discrimination to me.\n\n  JonSG\n\n  –\n  [JonSG](/users/218663/jonsg \"13,657 reputation\")\n\n  2025-01-08 15:31:45 +00:00\n\n  [Commented\n  Jan 8, 2025 at 15:31](#comment1014406_430298)\n* 1\n\n  @JonSG There is a big difference between asking age in a job hiring process - both because of the nature of the process (age discrimination when hiring is illegal, though hard to enforce, in many countries) and because of the personally identifiable aspect. In a large nominally-anonymous survey, age can be used as part of the analysis of data, particularly when that data (in aggregate) will be used for planning and marketing purposes.\n\n  manassehkatz-Moving 2 Codidact\n\n  –\n  [manassehkatz-Moving 2 Codidact](/users/2475129/manassehkatz-moving-2-codidact \"3,064 reputation\")\n\n  2025-01-08 15:40:17 +00:00\n\n  [Commented\n  Jan 8, 2025 at 15:40](#comment1014408_430298)\n* 1\n\n  Regarding Ukraine having a significantly higher percentage of participants compared to 2021, there were five times as many respondents from Ukraine in 2024, and US respondents were significantly fewer compared to 2021. So no, no *\"fake stats\"*. This isn't some form of propaganda. It's just how percentages work, when the numbers for other countries decrease.\n\n  Cerbrus\n\n  –\n  [Cerbrus](/users/1835379/cerbrus \"73,403 reputation\")\n\n  2025-07-13 09:52:21 +00:00\n\n  [Commented\n  Jul 13, 2025 at 9:52](#comment1029517_430298)\n\nAdd a comment\n |\n\n## 26 Answers 26\n\nSorted by:\n[Reset to useful\n\n148\n\nThis answer is not useful\n\nSave this answer.\n\nLoading when this answer was accepted…\n\nShow activity on this post.\n\nI am a bit disappointed that you only ask further AI related questions to people that either use AI for development work or plan to use it.\n\nIt is quite possible for someone to have a good knowledge of how AI works, what are its downsides and upsides, can evaluate its usefulness, and even more being able to answer whether they are afraid that AI will jeopardize their job or not while at the same time not using it for development and work.\n\nIf it is not possible for changing this part in the current survey, then please do that for the next year's survey.\n\n[Share](/a/430300)\n\nShare a link to this answer\n\nCopy link[CC BY-SA notifications\n\nanswered May 20, 2024 at 15:12\n\n[![Dalija Prasnikar's user avatar](https://i.sstatic.net/njcHD.jpg?s=64)](/users/4267244/dalija-prasnikar)\n\n[Dalija Prasnikar](/users/4267244/dalija-prasnikar)Mod\n\n28.7k1414 gold badges106106 silver badges148148 bronze badges\n\n12\n\n* 41\n\n  Indeed, right now my job involves *developing AI* but because we don't use AI tools that entire section was skipped.\n\n  rjzii\n\n  –\n  [rjzii](/users/1185/rjzii \"14,631 reputation\")\n\n  2024-05-20 15:14:38 +00:00\n\n  [Commented\n  May 20, 2024 at 15:14](#comment997447_430300)\n* 68\n\n  Why would they allow users who are more likely to rate it poorly fill out that section? that's bad for marketing\n\n  user400654\n\n  –\n  [user400654](/users/400654/user400654 \"95,112 reputation\")\n\n  2024-05-20 15:32:14 +00:00\n\n  [Commented\n  May 20, 2024 at 15:32](#comment997449_430300)\n* 19\n\n  @KevinB Obviously it depends on what the questions in that section actually are, but if I was designing a survey to see how people are using AI as part of the development process, then I would also want to know why they aren't using AI as well. There's a big difference between \"I don't like the tools\" and \"Corporate policy doesn't allow the use of GenAI\" or \"The tools are too prone to hallucinations to be used in a secure computing environment.\"\n\n  rjzii\n\n  –\n  [rjzii](/users/1185/rjzii \"14,631 reputation\")\n\n  2024-05-20 15:59:17 +00:00\n\n  [Commented\n  May 20, 2024 at 15:59](#comment997450_430300)\n* 16\n\n  As the survey denotes that the user *doesn't* use AI, then they can easily filter those results out, @KevinB . Plus, someone might *not* use AI because they had a terrible experience with it and therefore intentionally *avoid* it; those opinions do matter even if [Stack Overflow](https://stackoverflow.com) thinks they don't, as OverflowAI in no way ever [hallucinates](https://meta.stackoverflow.com/a/425167).\n\n  Thom A\n\n  –\n  [Thom A](/users/2029983/thom-a \"98,174 reputation\")\n  Mod\n\n  2024-05-20 17:27:02 +00:00\n\n  [Commented\n  May 20, 2024 at 17:27](#comment997455_430300)\n* 5\n\n  @ThomA I don’t disagree that it’d be valuable information, but let’s keep in mind what the purpose of this survey is and always has been.\n\n  user400654\n\n  –\n  [user400654](/users/400654/user400654 \"95,112 reputation\")\n\n  2024-05-20 17:38:38 +00:00\n\n  [Commented\n  May 20, 2024 at 17:38](#comment997458_430300)\n* 9\n\n  This. I actually went back and changed my answer just so I could answer the other AI questions. accepted…\n\nShow activity on this post.\n\nI know it can be skipped, but the question \"*Assign points to the following attributes of your job according to those that contribute the most to your satisfaction out of 100 total points*\" really needs to have an 'Other' option since the options provided make a lot of assumptions about what is motivating people.\n\nI've known more than a handful of developers over the years that would say the most satisfying thing is the paycheck, and there are others that care less about the tools and more about what they are producing (e.g., a game developer getting satisfaction from the game they shipped and how it was reviewed).\n\n[Share](/a/430299)\n\nShare a link to this answer\n\nCopy link[CC BY-SA 3\n\n  I think that the most reasonable understanding of this question (as asked) is that you are rating the attributes' contribution to your satisfaction *relative to the other listed attributes*. In that sense, \"Other\" would only make sense if you feel that none of the listed options contribute meaningfully to your satisfaction (which could be a reasonable response: perhaps one only cares about the money and nothing else; lots of people (not all!) in the financial sector have jobs that are only tolerable if that's the only thing one cares about...)\n\n  Ryan M\n\n  –\n  [Ryan M](/users/208273/ryan-m \"20,711 reputation\")\n  Mod\n\n  2024-05-21 00:17:54 +00:00\n\n  [Commented\n  May 21, 2024 at 0:17](#comment997491_430299)\n* 3\n\n  @RyanM That is one possible interpretation of the question, but it's bad survey design to make assumptions about how a respondent will interpret a question, practically considering the sample frame is an audience that's used to things being taken literally by the computer. :)\n\n  rjzii\n\n  –\n  [rjzii](/users/1185/rjzii \"14,631 reputation\")\n\n  2024-05-21 12:19:23 +00:00\n\n  [Commented\n  May 21, 2024 at 12:19](#comment997542_430299)\n* 2\n\n  Erm. Select all that apply.\n\nHow do I currently learn how to code (i.e. build upon my existing knowledge of how to code)? Or how have I gotten my knowledge of how to code to the point where it's at?\n\nAs someone who has been coding for multiple decades, the answers to the two are very different.\n\nI learned to code primarily from physical books, and from university courses, and from reading API documentation. I almost never do the first any more, I'm not in university so I don't do the second, and I do the third but it's more about making me more aware of existing and new features in the ecosystem and not \"learning to code\".\n\nProfessionally, I've learned much of what I've needed to know day-to-day by Googling it and reading about it on Stack Overflow and blogs. And that's currently my main way of finding stuff out.\n\nIt's arguable that I'm not even really \"learning to code\" at this point in my life, so much as learning new frameworks and technologies and fitting them into my already existing knowledge of \"how to code\".\n\n[Share](/a/430306)\n\nShare a link to this answer\n\nCopy link[CC BY-SA 4.0](https://creativecommons.org/licenses/by-sa/4.0/ \"The current license for this post: CC BY-SA 4.0\")\n\nShort permalink to this answer\n\nFollow\n\nFollow this answer to receive notifications\n\n[edited May 20, 2024 at 21:11](/posts/430306/revisions \"show all edits to this post\")\n\nanswered May 20, 2024 at 20:57\n\n[![M. Select all that apply.\"* [has, as one of the options, \"Overflow API\"](https://i.sstatic.net/ezUPFpvI.png).\n\nThere is no site or product by this exact name. It may refer to:\n\n* [This page promoting OverflowAPI](https://stackoverflow.co/api-solutions/) (note: no space), \"a subscription-based API service that provides continuous access to Stack Overflow’s public dataset to train and fine-tune large language models.\" However, that doesn't exactly seem like a \"site\".\n* Whatever non-public documentation, if any, may exist for the OverflowAPI product described on that page.\n* The [Stack Exchange API](https://api.stackexchange.com/docs). This would make the most sense to ask about as a site that one might have visited in a public survey, but it is the furthest from the name used.\n\n[Share](/a/430308)\n\nShare a link to this answer\n\nCopy link[CC BY-SA 4.0](https://creativecommons.org/licenses/by-sa/4.0/ \"The current license for this post: CC BY-SA 4.0\")\n\nShort permalink to this answer\n\nFollow\n\nFollow this answer to receive notifications\n\n[edited May 21, 2024 at 1:14](/posts/430308/revisions \"show all edits to this post\")\n\nanswered May 20, 2024 at 22:37\n\n[![Ryan M's user avatar](https://www.gravatar.com/avatar/65129b14ad4a755ba297a84f0ea03727?s=64&d=identicon&r=PG)](/users/208273/ryan-m)\n\n[Ryan M](/users/208273/ryan-m)Mod\n\n20.7k1212 gold badges202202 silver badges236236 bronze badges\n\n2\n\n* If it's the SE API, it'd be great if it could be clarified if the survey meant whether a user visited a *documentation* page for it or whether they've accessed an API *endpoint*. Select all that apply.\n\nI am strongly missing options for anything related to **quality assurance**.\n\nOr was it left out, because too many would check it, if even just out of resentment?\n\nJokes aside, in my company, QA *is* a major challenge, because we are doing archive migrations. Therefore, we have to interface with legacy systems that we often don't have direct access to in early development phases and usually our options for deployment are quite limited, too. This make it *really* hard to assess the correctness of anything we build, which then usually spreads to other departments in the company, because for example how do you plan a project with that?\n\n[Share](/a/430345)\n\nShare a link to this answer\n\nCopy link[CC BY-SA 4.0](https://creativecommons.org/licenses/by-sa/4.0/ \"The current license for this post: CC BY-SA 4.0\")\n\nShort permalink to this answer\n\nFollow\n\nFollow this answer to receive notifications\n\nanswered May 23, 2024 at 18:25\n\n[![Izruo's user avatar](https://i.sstatic.net/apSzB.jpg?s=64)](/users/7525132/izruo)\n\n[Izruo](/users/7525132/izruo)\n\n2,30699 silver badges44 bronze badges\n\nAdd a comment\n |\n\nThis answer is useful\n\n6\n\nThis answer is not useful\n\nSave this answer.\n\nLoading when this answer was accepted…\n\nShow activity on this post.\n\nA question asks\n\n> What is the source of the technical documentation you use most often to learn to code?\n> Select all that apply.\n\nAnd these are the possible answers:\n\n1. I still have not gone through the survey completely, so here just few ones I noticed:\n\n* (On Firefox) Charts with multiple tabs do not show any data until you switch between the tabs.\n* Clicking on \"community\" on the main page tiles leads to an error page [GIF of \"community is undefined\"](https://i.sstatic.net/82FBqj9T.gif).\n\n  + The problem is that the URL is apparently case-sensitive: clicking the tile leads to `https://survey.stackoverflow.co/2024/Community/` with a capital \"C\" which does not work. While `https://survey.stackoverflow.co/2024/community/` with a lowercase \"C\" does work.\n* Some charts show very suspicious numbers of responses that I do not believe can be correct. For example in [\"Frequency of participation on Stack Overflow\"](https://survey.stackoverflow.co/2024/community#participation-and-feedback-so-part-freq) there are four tabs, each has the number of responses in the lower right. Here they are:\n\n  + All Respondents: **45,237 (69.1%)**\n  + Professional Developers: **4,139 (6.3%)**\n  + Learning to Code: **4,139 (6.3%)**\n  + Other Coders: **45,237 (69.1%)**\n\n    I find it *extremely unlikely* that \"Professional\" and \"Learning\" have the exact same number of responses. Even if they do, I do not think it possible that \"All\" and \"Other\" are the same.\n    I found a similar thing in other questions - the *exact* same number of responses for two different tabs, however I cannot reliably reproduce it. I think the \"Frequency of participation on Stack Overflow\" question has consistently wrong.\n* The \"Previous\" link at the bottom goes to the current page you're on. For example, if you are in the \"Work\" section, the Previous link leads to \"Work\". The Next link does go to the next section.\n\nThis is an incomplete list of just few things I noticed.\n\n[Share](/a/431108)\n\nShare a link to this answer\n\nCopy link[CC BY-SA 4.0](https://creativecommons.org/licenses/by-sa/4.0/ \"The current license for this post: CC BY-SA 4.0\")\n\nShort permalink to this answer\n\nFollow\n\nFollow this answer to receive notifications\n\n[edited Jul 29, 2024 at 8:58](/posts/431108/revisions \"show all edits to this post\")\n\nanswered Jul 26, 2024 at 7:11\n\n[![VLAZ's user avatar](https://i.sstatic.net/hy0Bnl.png?s=64)](/users/3689450/vlaz)\n\n[VLAZ](/users/3689450/vlaz)\n\n29.8k99 gold badges136136 silver badges207207 bronze badges\n\n3\n\n* Thank you for flagging some issues with the survey site here. For example:\n\n> Which embedded systems and technologies have you done extensive development work in over the past year, and which do you want to work in over the next year?\n\nIn the last year I've been working on a project that is built using QMake (which is on the list). Do I do extensive development with it? I'm not sure what you mean!\n\nSometimes I compile tens of times per day. And yet, I've never modified a single .pro file, except for really trivial stuff, like adding a new source file to the list of the existing ones. I've never changed compiler flags, linker flags, options, paths, dependencies, anything: all of these things were set up by others long before I joined the project.\n\nSo QMake is used in my project, yes, but not really by me, or at most in a very passive way. Does that count as \"doing extensive development work with it\"?\n\n[Share](/a/430432)\n\nShare a link to this answer\n\nCopy link[CC BY-SA 4.0](https://creativecommons.org/licenses/by-sa/4.0/ \"The current license for this post: CC BY-SA 4.0\")\n\nShort permalink to this answer\n\nFollow\n\nFollow this answer to receive notifications\n\nanswered May 30, 2024 at 22:23\n\n[![Fabio says Reinstate Monica's user avatar](https://i.sstatic.net/rX1ss.png?s=64)](/users/3982001/fabio-says-reinstate-monica)\n\n[Fabio says Reinstate Monica](/users/3982001/fabio-says-reinstate-monica)\n\n5,9071818 silver badges2121 bronze badges\n\nAdd a comment\n |\n\nThis answer is useful\n\n4\n\nThis answer is not useful\n\nSave this answer.\n\nLoading when this answer was accepted…\n\nShow activity on this post.\n\nOn the questions about what language/framework/etc I use now vs what I want to be using in a year, the “want” always confuses me. Does it really mean “want”, as in even if I stop working I’d continue to play around with it or if I switch jobs I’d try to introduce it at my new employer, or is it more what will I be working with in a year, which could be because I want to, or more likely, because that’s what the company I work for uses?\n\n[Share](/a/430304)\n\nShare a link to this answer\n\nCopy link[CC BY-SA 4.0](https://creativecommons.org/licenses/by-sa/4.0/ \"The current license for this post: CC BY-SA 4.0\")\n\nShort permalink to this answer\n\nFollow\n\nFollow this answer to receive notifications\n\nanswered May 20, 2024 at 17:12\n\n[![blm's user avatar](https://www.gravatar.com/avatar/8f3e40a478e3ef60ab183f4b0956bfa7?s=64&d=identicon&r=PG&f=y&so-version=2)](/users/5349263/blm)\n\n[blm](/users/5349263/blm)\n\n2,4941010 silver badges33 bronze badges\n\n4\n\n* 4\n\n  “Wants” are rarely independent of all other considerations. I may want to work with a tool because I genuinely like it. I may want to work with a tool to keep current and increase my future employment opportunities. I may want to work with a tool because it’s what my current employer uses and I like where I’m working, even if I don’t particularly like the tool. Or some combination of those.\n\n  blm\n\n  –\n  [blm](/users/5349263/blm \"2,494 reputation\")\n\n  2024-05-20 17:39:53 +00:00\n\n  [Commented\n  May 20, 2024 at 17:39](#comment997459_430304)\n* 3\n\n  Who knows. For years they’ve used this line of questioning to determine what languages developers like/hate, despite not indicating that in the question at all.\n\n  user400654\n\n  –\n  [user400654](/users/400654/user400654 \"95,112 reputation\")\n\n  2024-05-20 17:45:00 +00:00\n\n  [Commented\n  May 20, 2024 at 17:45](#comment997460_430304)\n* 14\n\n  It doesn't need to be indicated in the question. The problem is that they're analysing the data incorrectly. They treat not picking \"want\" as \"don't want\" when it more often than not doesn't mean that. There's plenty of things I've used in the past year that I don't *want* to work with, but also don't want to *avoid* working with. They're applying the wrong meaning to the \"want\" checkbox, and have been for years. This has also been commented on several times in the past, and they so far haven't cared. We'll see if this year's analysis is any different, but I strongly doubt it.\n\n  Zoe - Save the data dump\n\n  –\n  [Zoe - Save the data dump](/users/6296561/zoe-save-the-data-dump \"28,402 reputation\")\n  Mod\n\n  2024-05-20 17:49:22 +00:00\n\n  [Commented\n  May 20, 2024 at 17:49](#comment997461_430304)\n* 1\n\n  It's just two worded completely uselessly, so [they can make up whatever meaning they want](https://meta.stackoverflow.com/a/418010), rather than what the users might mean.\n\n  VLAZ\n\n  –\n  [VLAZ](/users/3689450/vlaz \"29,843 reputation\")\n\n  2024-05-20 19:42:18 +00:00\n\n  [Commented\n  May 20, 2024 at 19:42](#comment997464_430304)\n\nAdd a comment\n |\n\nThis answer is useful\n\n3\n\nThis answer is not useful\n\nSave this answer.\n\nLoading when this answer was accepted…\n\nShow activity on this post.\n\nThe question regarding the compensation, it does not mention which currency I should input my compensation in. Though it asks for my currency, it does not make it obvious whether or not the currency I choose here is the one to be considered for the compensation field.\n\nDoes anyone *know* what currency applies to the compensation field?\n\n[![compensation-question](https://i.sstatic.net/65MdMU1B.png)](https://i.sstatic.net/65MdMU1B.png)\n\n[Share](/a/430319)\n\nShare a link to this answer\n\nCopy link[CC BY-SA 4.0](https://creativecommons.org/licenses/by-sa/4.0/ \"The current license for this post: CC BY-SA 4.0\")\n\nShort permalink to this answer\n\nFollow\n\nFollow this answer to receive notifications\n\nanswered May 21, 2024 at 9:35\n\n[![Sнаđошƒаӽ's user avatar](https://i.sstatic.net/fUsUA.png?s=64)](/users/3375713/s%D0%BD%D0%B0%C4%91%D0%BE%D1%88%C6%92%D0%B0%D3%BD)\n\n[Sнаđошƒаӽ](/users/3375713/s%D0%BD%D0%B0%C4%91%D0%BE%D1%88%C6%92%D0%B0%D3%BD)\n\n17.9k1313 silver badges1414 bronze badges\n\n6\n\n* 8\n\n  Judging by the wording preceding the currency selection (\"*estimating in*\"), and the wording of the compensation input box (\"*please estimate*\") as well as my own common sense - I'd say, while it is not *explicit*, it *is* fairly *obvious* that the currency you chose is what you're expected to use in the number field.\n\n  Dev-iL\n\n  –\n  [Dev-iL](/users/3372061/dev-il \"24,329 reputation\")\n\n  2024-05-21 09:58:22 +00:00\n\n  [Commented\n  May 21, 2024 at 9:58](#comment997531_430319)\n* 22\n\n  I am also 97.8% sure that the currency selected in the dropdown above is the one to be considered for the compensation field. But I would still maintain that the wording should been clearer, sth along the lines of \"What is your current total annual compensation in the currency you selected above...\". All I am saying is when making a survey that is going to be taken by millions, everything should be stated as clearly as possible.\n\n  Sнаđошƒаӽ\n\n  –\n  [Sнаđошƒаӽ](/users/3375713/s%D0%BD%D0%B0%C4%91%D0%BE%D1%88%C6%92%D0%B0%D3%BD \"17,880 reputation\")\n\n  2024-05-21 10:12:25 +00:00\n\n  [Commented\n  May 21, 2024 at 10:12](#comment997535_430319)\n* 7\n\n  This is a very good point. It's worrying to see that this has been downvoted on a developer site. The Design of Everyday Things should be a mandatory reading. The problem is not whether it's obvious or not, but the very fact that you even have to pause to think about it is bad UX.\n\n  Maciej Kravchyk\n\n  –\n  [Maciej Kravchyk](/users/4925661/maciej-kravchyk \"17,113 reputation\")\n\n  2024-05-22 10:22:03 +00:00\n\n  [Commented\n  May 22, 2024 at 10:22](#comment997686_430319)\n* @MaciejKravchyk Exactly! Those who downvoted this are probably not very precise in their communications and probably don't care about being precise either.\n\n  Sнаđошƒаӽ\n\n  –\n  [Sнаđошƒаӽ](/users/3375713/s%D0%BD%D0%B0%C4%91%D0%BE%D1%88%C6%92%D0%B0%D3%BD \"17,880 reputation\")\n\n  2024-05-23 09:19:51 +00:00\n\n  [Commented\n  May 23, 2024 at 9:19](#comment997788_430319)\n* 2\n\n  I also decided it could obviously only be in the currency I had just chosen but I did wonder since it wasn't explicitly mentioned.\n\n  Félix Adriyel Gagnon-Grenier\n\n  –\n  [Félix Adriyel Gagnon-Grenier](/users/576767/f%C3%A9lix-adriyel-gagnon-grenier \"8,956 reputation\")\n\n  2024-05-23 19:03:37 +00:00\n\n  [Commented\n  May 23, 2024 at 19:03](#comment997817_430319)\n* @MaciejKravchyk Indeed! One of the best timesavers I have learned is how to answer questions like \"on a scale of 1 to 10 ...\" No need to think about whether it is a 3 or a 4 just answer either 1 or 10. The intermediate answers are fairly useless to the question setter anyway.\n\n  MT1\n\n  –\n  [MT1](/users/10186832/mt1 \"955 reputation\")\n\n  2024-05-31 04:40:05 +00:00\n\n  [Commented\n  May 31, 2024 at 4:40](#comment998595_430319)\n\nAdd a comment\n |\n\nThis answer is useful\n\n1\n\nThis answer is not useful\n\nSave this answer.\n\nLoading when this answer was accepted…\n\nShow activity on this post.\n\nOn this question:\n\n> Which of the following describes your current job, the one you do most\n> of the time? Please select only one.\n\nThere isn't anything between an `Engineering Manager` and `Senior Executive (C-Suite, VP, etc)`. In my opinion a director role does not fit well in either of this. I used the `Other (please specify):` option, but I guess this is a somewhat common answer that deserves its radio button (and this would make sure people don't write it out differently).\n\n[Share](/a/430427)\n\nShare a link to this answer\n\nCopy link[CC BY-SA 4.0](https://creativecommons.org/licenses/by-sa/4.0/ \"The current license for this post: CC BY-SA 4.0\")\n\nShort permalink to this answer\n\nFollow\n\nFollow this answer to receive notifications\n\nanswered May 30, 2024 at 12:56\n\n[![Ivaylo Strandjev's user avatar](https://www.gravatar.com/avatar/732c48dee9ac15216258118fb8c0a764?s=64&d=identicon&r=PG)](/users/812912/ivaylo-strandjev)\n\n[Ivaylo Strandjev](/users/812912/ivaylo-strandjev)\n\n71.4k11 gold badge2020 silver badges1717 bronze badges\n\nAdd a comment\n |\n\nThis answer is useful\n\n0\n\nThis answer is not useful\n\nSave this answer.\n\nLoading when this answer was accepted…\n\nShow activity on this post.\n\nI can take the survey twice! If it is, why?\n\n[Share](/a/430349)\n\nShare a link to this answer\n\nCopy link[CC BY-SA 4.0](https://creativecommons.org/licenses/by-sa/4.0/ \"The current license for this post: CC BY-SA 4.0\")\n\nShort permalink to this answer\n\nFollow\n\nFollow this answer to receive notifications\n\nanswered May 24, 2024 at 5:51\n\n[![Sнаđошƒаӽ's user avatar](https://i.sstatic.net/fUsUA.png?s=64)](/users/3375713/s%D0%BD%D0%B0%C4%91%D0%BE%D1%88%C6%92%D0%B0%D3%BD)\n\n[Sнаđошƒаӽ](/users/3375713/s%D0%BD%D0%B0%C4%91%D0%BE%D1%88%C6%92%D0%B0%D3%BD)\n\n17.9k1313 silver badges1414 bronze badges\n\n2\n\n* 10\n\n  probably not quite \"by design\" in the sense of \"yes, we really want you to do this!\". more like- I don't see how they could totally prevent you from doing this. even if you had to log into something to submit a response, you could just make multiple accounts. maybe simple cases could be prevented by setting a flag in localstorage that you've submitted a response to this particular survey already on this machine (even then, it's not hard to edit localstorage).\n\n  starball\n\n  –\n  [starball](/users/11107541/starball \"61,868 reputation\")\n  Mod\n\n  2024-05-24 08:47:46 +00:00\n\n  [Commented\n  May 24, 2024 at 8:47](#comment997877_430349)\n* 1\n\n  @starball: well, while we can't probably prevent developers from answering multiple times, it would have been nice to set a cookie (or similar) that I already answered.     {
      "title": "2025 Stack Overflow Developer Survey",
      "description": "It saw a 7 percentage point increase from 2024 to 2025; be the go-to language for AI, data science, and back-end development. Company info Company size 4.3.",
      "url": "https://survey.stackoverflow.co/2025",
      "content": "![Stack Overflow Developer Survey 2025](/2025/img/logo.svg)\n\nProducts\n\n1. [Stack Overflow Where developers and technologists go to gain and share knowledge.](https://stackoverflow.com/?utm_medium=referral&utm_source=survey&utm_campaign=dev-survey-2025&utm_content=pubplat)\n2. [Stack Overflow for Teams Where developers & technologists share private knowledge with\n   coworkers](https://stackoverflow.co/teams/?utm_medium=referral&utm_source=survey&utm_campaign=dev-survey-2025&utm_content=teams-dropdown)\n3. [Advertising Reach devs & technologists worldwide about your product, service or\n   employer brand](https://stackoverflow.co/advertising/?utm_medium=referral&utm_source=survey&utm_campaign=dev-survey-2025&utm_content=advertising-dropdown)\n4. [Knowledge Solutions Data licensing offering for businesses to build and improve AI\n   tools and models](https://stackoverflow.co/api-solutions/?utm_medium=referral&utm_source=survey&utm_campaign=dev-survey-2025&utm_content=data-dropdown)\n5. [Labs The future of collective knowledge sharing](https://stackoverflow.co/labs/?utm_medium=referral&utm_source=survey&utm_campaign=dev-survey-2025&utm_content=labs-dropdown)\n6. [About the company](https://stackoverflow.co/?utm_medium=referral&utm_source=survey&utm_campaign=dev-survey-2025&utm_content=company-dropdown) [Visit the blog](https://stackoverflow.blog/?utm_medium=referral&utm_source=survey&utm_campaign=dev-survey-2025&utm_content=blog-dropdown)\n\nThe 2025 Developer Survey is the definitive report on the state of software development. In its fifteenth year, Stack Overflow received over 49,000+ responses from 177 countries across 62 questions focused on 314 different technologies, including new focus on AI agent tools, LLMs and community platforms. This annual Developer Survey provides a crucial snapshot into the needs of the global developer community, focusing on the tools and technologies they use or want to learn more about.\n\n![](/2025/img/hero.jpg)\n\n1. [Developers](/2025/developers/)\n2. [Technology](/2025/technology/)\n3. [AI](/2025/ai/)\n4. [Work](/2025/work/)\n5. [Stack Overflow](/2025/stack-overflow/)\n6. [Methodology](/2025/methodology/)\n\n[Developers](/2025/developers/) → [Education & Experience](/2025/developers#education-experience)\n\n## More than one third of respondents use AI-enabled tools to learn AI this year\n\nAll RespondentsYes, I learned how to use AI-enabled tools required for my job or to benefit my career36.3%Yes, I learned how to use AI-enabled tools for my personal curiosity and/or hobbies31%No, I learned something that was not related to AI or AI enablement for my personal curiosity and/or hobbies11.7%No, I didn't spend time learning in the past year10.8%No, I learned something that was not related to AI or AI enablement as required for my job or to benefit my career10.2%\n\nOver 36% of respondents learned how to use AI-enabled tools for their job or to advance their career in the last year.\n\n[Learn to code for AI →](/2025/developers#2-learn-to-code-for-ai)\n\n[Developers](/2025/developers/) → [Education & Experience](/2025/developers#education-experience)\n\n## Most developers have been coding for 10+ years\n\nAll Respondents1 to 5 years13.9%6 to 10 years21.1%11 to 15 years15.6%16 to 20 years11.8%21 to 30 years14.6%31 to 40 years7%41 to 50 years3.1%More than 50 years0.5%\n\n35% of developers responding to the Developer Survey this year have been coding less than 10 years.\n\n[Years coding →](/2025/developers#2-years-coding)\n\n[Technology](/2025/technology/) → [Admired and Desired](/2025/technology#admired-and-desired)\n\n## GitHub is a more desirable collaboration tool than Jira this year\n\nDesired and Admired59.3%70.1%GitHub27%75.8%Markdown File25.6%59.5%GitLab22%42.1%Jira14.3%40.2%ConfluenceDesiredAdmired\n\nJira steps down as the most desired tool for code documentation and collaboration and the new top desired tool is GitHub. Markdown continues to be the most admired sync tool for the third year.\n\n[Code documentation and collaboration tools →](/2025/technology#2-code-documentation-and-collaboration-tools)\n\n[Technology](/2025/technology/) → [Most popular technologies](/2025/technology#most-popular-technologies)\n\n## A vast majority of developers indicating they worked with OpenAI GPT models in the past year\n\nAll RespondentsOpenAI GPT81.4%Claude Sonnet42.8%Gemini Flash35.3%OpenAI Reasoning34.6%OpenAI Image26.6%\n\nOpenAI's GPT models top the large language model list with 82% of developers indicating they used them for development work in the past year. Anthropic's Claude Sonnet models are used more by professional developers (45%) than by those learning to code (30%).\n\n[Large language models →](/2025/technology#1-large-language-models)\n\n[AI](/2025/ai/) → [AI Agents](/2025/ai#ai-agents)\n\n## 69% of AI agent users agree AI agents have increased productivity\n\nAll Respondents27.3%35.9%21.3%8.2%7.3%AI agents have accelerated my learning about new technologies or codebases.29.3%34.9%22.4%7%6.4%AI agents have helped me automate repetitive tasks.17.1%31.9%25.3%14.2%11.5%AI agents have helped me solve complex problems more effectively.6.6%10.7%40.5%20%22.2%AI agents have improved collaboration within my team.12.2%25.3%32.4%17.1%13.1%AI agents have improved the quality of my code.27.7%41%20.4%6%4.9%AI agents have increased my productivity.29.3%40.8%17.8%6.9%5.1%AI agents have reduced the time spent on specific development tasks.Strongly agreeSomewhat agreeNeutralSomewhat disagreeStrongly disagree\n\nThe most recognized impacts are personal efficiency gains, and not team-wide impact. Approximately 70% of agent users agree that agents have reduced the time spent on specific development tasks, and 69% agree they have increased productivity. Only 17% of users agree that agents have improved collaboration within their team, making it the lowest-rated impact by a wide margin.\n\n[Impacts of AI agents →](/2025/ai#3-impacts-of-ai-agents)\n\n[AI](/2025/ai/) → [Sentiment and usage](/2025/ai#sentiment-and-usage)\n\n## 84% of respondents are using AI tools this year\n\nAll RespondentsYes, I use AI tools daily47.1%Yes, I use AI tools weekly17.7%Yes, I use AI tools monthly or infrequently13.7%No, but I plan to soon5.3%No, and I don't plan to16.2%\n\n84% of respondents are using or planning to use AI tools in their development process, an increase over last year (76%). This year we can see 51% of professional developers use AI tools daily.\n\n[AI tools in the development process →](/2025/ai#1-ai-tools-in-the-development-process)\n\n[AI](/2025/ai/) → [Developer tools](/2025/ai#developer-tools)\n\n## 66% of developers are frustrated with AI solutions that are almost right\n\nAll RespondentsAI solutions that are almost right, but not quite66%Debugging AI-generated code is more time-consuming45.2%I don’t use AI tools regularly23.5%I’ve become less confident in my own problem-solving20%It’s hard to understand how or why the code works16.3%Other (write in):11.6%I haven’t encountered any problems4%\n\nThe biggest single frustration, cited by 66% of developers, is dealing with \"AI solutions that are almost right, but not quite,\" which often leads to the second-biggest frustration: \"Debugging AI-generated code is more time-consuming\" (45%)\n\n[AI tool frustrations →](/2025/ai#2-ai-tool-frustrations)\n\n[Technology](/2025/technology/) → [Admired and Desired](/2025/technology#admired-and-desired)\n\n## Cargo is the most admired cloud development and infrastructure tool this year\n\nDesired and Admired15.7%51.8%Terraform15.2%56.4%Homebrew13.9%70.8%Cargo12.6%47.6%Make11.5%58.8%APTDesiredAdmired\n\nRust's growth is directly tied to the success of its build tool and package manager, Cargo, which is the most admired (71%) cloud development and infrastructure tool this year.\n\n[Cloud development →](/2025/technology#2-cloud-development)\n\n[Developers](/2025/developers/) → [Developer roles](/2025/developers#developer-roles)\n\n## Architect is the fourth top role for developers this year\n\nAll RespondentsDeveloper, full-stack27%Developer, back-end14.2%Student11.3%Architect, software or solutions6.1%Developer, desktop or enterprise applications4.3%\n\nArchitect is a new role we added to the survey this year and is the fourth most popular role for respondents.\n\n[Role →](/2025/developers#3-role)\n\n[Developers](/2025/developers/) → [Profile](/2025/developers#profile)\n\n## USA, Germany and India are top countries responding to this year's survey\n\nTop 10 CountriesUnited States of America20.4%Germany8.6%India7.2%United Kingdom of Great Britain and Northern Ireland5.8%France4%Canada3.7%Ukraine2.7%Poland2.5%Netherlands2.5%Italy2.4%\n\nUkraine and France swapped places this year compared to last, placing France in the top 5 list of responding countries.\n\n[Country →](/2025/developers#1-country)\n\n[Stack Overflow](/2025/stack-overflow/) → [Stack Overflow site use](/2025/stack-overflow#stack-overflow-site-use)\n\n## Stack Overflow is a destination developers visit frequently\n\nAll RespondentsMultiple times per day8.2%Daily or almost daily16.7%A few times per week28%A few times per month or weekly29.5%Less than once per month or monthly10.7%Less than once every 2 - 3 months4.1%Infrequently, less than once per year2.9%\n\nStack Overflow is a frequent destination for information. A strong majority (82%) visit at least a few times per month, with 25% visiting daily or more often.\n\n[Frequency of visiting Stack Overflow →](/2025/stack-overflow#1-frequency-of-visiting-stack-overflow)\n\n[AI](/2025/ai/) → [AI Agents](/2025/ai#ai-agents)\n\n## A majority of developers don't use AI agents\n\nAll RespondentsYes, I use AI agents at work daily14.1%Yes, I use AI agents at work weekly9%Yes, I use AI agents at work monthly or infrequently7.8%No, but I plan to17.4%No, I use AI exclusively in copilot/autocomplete mode13.8%No, and I don't plan to37.9%\n\nAI agents are not yet mainstream. A majority of developers (52%) either don't use agents or stick to simpler AI tools, and a significant portion (38%) have no plans to adopt them.\n\n[AI agents →](/2025/ai#3-ai-agents)\n\n[Work](/2025/work/) → [Technology purchases](/2025/work#technology-purchases)\n\n## Privacy, pricing and better alternatives are top reasons developers turn their back on a technology\n\n| Detraction | Overall Rank | Median Rank | Mode Rank |\n| --- | --- | --- | --- |\n| Security or privacy concerns | 1 | 3 | 1 |\n| Prohibitive pricing | 2 | 4 | 1 |\n| Availability of better alternatives | 3 | 4 | 1 |\n| Poor usability | 4 | 4 | 4 |\n| Inefficient or time-costly | 5 | 5 | 5 |\n| Outdated or obsolete technology or features | 6 | 5 | 8 |\n| Ethical concerns | 7 | 6 | 8 |\n| Lack of or sub-par API | 8 | 6 | 8 |\n| Lack of AI or AI agents | 9 | 9 | 9 |\n| Other | 10 | 10 | 10 |\n\nThe reasons to reject a technology are nearly universal. The top three deal-breakers for all developers are security or privacy concerns (Rank 1), prohibitive pricing (Rank 2), and the availability of better alternatives (Rank 3). The lack of AI is the least important factor (Rank 9).\n\n[How you lose interest in tech tools →](/2025/work#4-how-you-lose-interest-in-tech-tools)\n\n[Technology](/2025/technology/) → [Worked with vs. want to work with](/2025/technology#worked-with-vs-want-to-work-with)\n\n## Developers at all levels are exploring the evolving AI landscape through Stack Overflow\n\nAll RespondentsNo descriptionGoogle Gemini.NET 8+Large language modelOllamaTailwind CSS 4PydanticRAGShadcn/uiuvLangGraphc++23\n\nMost professional developers who indicated they used Google Gemini last year, are interested in other AI-oriented subjects like \"Large Language Model\" or \"RAG\", and tools like \"Ollama\".\n\nThis pattern holds true for both Professional Developers and those Learning to Code, showing that developers at all levels are actively exploring the rapidly evolving AI landscape rather than committing to a single tool or platform.\n\n[Stack Overflow tags →](/2025/technology#3-stack-overflow-tags)\n\n[AI](/2025/ai/) → [Developer tools](/2025/ai#developer-tools)\n\n## More developers actively distrust the accuracy of AI tools than trust it\n\nAll RespondentsHighly trust3.1%Somewhat trust29.6%Somewhat distrust26.1%Highly distrust19.6%\n\nMore developers actively distrust the accuracy of AI tools (46%) than trust it (33%), and only a fraction (3%) report \"highly trusting\" the output. Experienced developers are the most cautious, with the lowest \"highly trust\" rate (2.6%) and the highest \"highly distrust\" rate (20%), indicating a widespread need for human verification for those in roles with accountability.\n\n[Accuracy of AI tools →](/2025/ai#2-accuracy-of-ai-tools)\n\n[Technology](/2025/technology/) → [Most popular technologies](/2025/technology#most-popular-technologies)\n\n## Respondents learning to code use YouTube for community more than professional developers\n\nAll RespondentsStack Overflow84.2%GitHub (public)66.9%YouTube60.5%Reddit53.7%Stack Exchange46.5%\n\nRespondents learning to code use Youtube for community more than professional developers (70% vs. 60%).\n\n[Community platforms →](/2025/technology#1-community-platforms)\n\n[Technology](/2025/technology/) → [Admired and Desired](/2025/technology#admired-and-desired)\n\n## uv is the most admired SO tag technology this year\n\nDesired and Admired13.9%61.4%RAG12.4%65.1%c++2311.3%74.2%uv9.4%62.6%Shadcn/ui9.2%61.2%PydanticDesiredAdmired\n\nuv is a Python package manager built in Rust; need we say more about why this is the most admired (74%) SO tag technology this year?\n\n[Stack Overflow tags →](/2025/technology#2-stack-overflow-tags)\n\n[Stack Overflow](/2025/stack-overflow/) → [Participation and Feedback](/2025/stack-overflow#participation-and-feedback)\n\n## Stack Overflow is becoming a new resource for developers that need to solve AI-related issues\n\nAll RespondentsRarely, almost never42.9%I don't use AI or AI-enabled tools22.1%Less than half of the time18.5%About half of the time9%More than half the time7.4%\n\nDevelopers turn to Stack Overflow for human-verified, trusted knowledge. About 35% of developers report that their visits to Stack Overflow are a result of AI-related issues at least some of the time.\n\n[Frictions on Stack Overflow →](/2025/stack-overflow#2-frictions-on-stack-overflow)\n\n[Work](/2025/work/) → [Employment](/2025/work#employment)\n\n## Nearly one third of developers are working remote this year\n\nAll RespondentsRemote32.4%Your choice (very flexible, you can come in when you want or just as needed)12.6%In-person17.9%Hybrid (some remote, leans heavy to in-person)19.9%Hybrid (some in-person, leans heavy to flexibility)17.2%\n\nOf the top-reporting countries in this year's survey, the US has the highest number of developers working remotely (45%). 21% of developers in Germany say the choice to go into the office or work remotely is completely up to them.\n\n[Work environment →](/2025/work#1-work-environment)\n\n[Technology](/2025/technology/) → [Admired and Desired](/2025/technology#admired-and-desired)\n\n## Claude Sonnet is the most admired AI model\n\nDesired and Admired51.2%61.2%OpenAI GPT33.3%67.5%Claude Sonnet25.9%63.6%OpenAI Reasoning24%56.6%Gemini Flash22.7%65.2%Gemini ReasoningDesiredAdmired\n\nAnthropic's Claude Sonnet is the most admired LLM this year (behind Gemini Reasoning) and second most desired (33%).\n\n[Large language models →](/2025/technology#2-large-language-models)\n\n[Work](/2025/work/) → [Job Satisfaction](/2025/work#job-satisfaction)\n\n## One in four developers are happy at their current job\n\nAll RespondentsNot Happy at Work28.4%Complacent at Work47.1%Happy at Work24.5%\n\nMore developers are happy at work this year (24% vs. 20% last year). This is likely related to that pay bump in the data for certain roles this year.\n\n[Job satisfaction →](/2025/work#5-job-satisfaction)\n\n[Technology](/2025/technology/) → [Most popular technologies](/2025/technology#most-popular-technologies)\n\n## Python adoption grew in 2025\n\nAll RespondentsJavaScript66%HTML/CSS61.9%SQL58.6%Python57.9%Bash/Shell48.7%\n\nAfter more than a decade of steady growth, Python's adoption has accelerated significantly. It saw a 7 percentage point increase from 2024 to 2025; this speaks to its ability to be the go-to language for AI, data science, and back-end development.\n\n[Programming, scripting, and markup languages →](/2025/technology#1-programming-scripting-and-markup-languages)\n\n[Stack Overflow](/2025/stack-overflow/) → [Participation and Feedback](/2025/stack-overflow#participation-and-feedback)\n\n## Younger developers want developer content with social or interactive formats\n\nAll RespondentsLists of recommendations (tools, frameworks, technologies, etc.)47.6%Long-form articles40.8%Chat (bot/AI)33.2%Coding challenges29.5%Chat (people)26.6%Videos25%Job board20.8%Day-in-the-life profiles/interviews with experts19%Message Boards17.8%Logic games/puzzles15.8%Direct messaging of users15%Other4.5%\n\nWhile all age groups want lists and articles, younger developers show a significantly higher interest in more social and interactive formats. For example, 37% of 18-24 year olds want \"Chat (people)\", compared to only 20% of 55-64 year olds. Similarly, 39% of the youngest cohort want \"Coding challenges,\" also aligning with a motivation to skill up.\n\n[How do you choose to find relevant developer content? →](/2025/stack-overflow#2-how-do-you-choose-to-find-relevant-developer-content)\n\n[Technology](/2025/technology/) → [Most popular technologies](/2025/technology#most-popular-technologies)\n\n## Visual Studio and Visual Studio Code both maintained their top spots for developer environments used for the fourth year\n\nAll RespondentsVisual Studio Code75.9%Visual Studio29%Notepad++27.4%IntelliJ IDEA27.1%Vim24.3%\n\nSubscription-based, AI-enabled IDEs weren't able to topple the dominance of Visual Studio and Visual Studio Code this year. Both maintained their top spots for the fourth year while relying on extensions as optional, paid AI services.\n\n[Dev IDEs →](/2025/technology#1-dev-id-es)\n\n[AI](/2025/ai/) → [Sentiment and usage](/2025/ai#sentiment-and-usage)\n\n## Positive sentiment to AI tools has decreased in 2025\n\nAll RespondentsVery favorable22.9%Favorable36.8%Indifferent17.6%Unsure2.3%Unfavorable10.8%Very unfavorable9.6%\n\nConversely to usage, positive sentiment for AI tools has decreased in 2025: 70%+ in 2023 and 2024 to just 60% this year. Professionals show a higher overall favorable sentiment (61%) than those learning to code (53%).\n\n[AI tool sentiment →](/2025/ai#1-ai-tool-sentiment)\n\n# Survey index\n\n1. [1. Developers](/2025/developers/)\n   1. [1.1. Profile](/2025/developers#profile)\n      1. [Coding](/2025/developers#1-coding)\n      2. [Age](/2025/developers#1-age)\n      3. [Country](/2025/developers#1-country)\n   2. [1.2. Education & Experience](/2025/developers#education-experience)\n      1. [How did you learn to code?](/2025/developers#2-how-did-you-learn-to-code)\n      5. [Learn to code for AI](/2025/developers#2-learn-to-code-for-ai)\n      6. They explore the decline in trust in AI tools, shifts in popular programming technologies, and the patterns Erin saw in salary growth among developers.\n\n![Image of Phoebe 6, 2024[# Unpacking the 2024 Developer Survey results](/2024/08/06/2024-developer-survey/)\n\nRyan and Eira talk with Stack Overflow senior research analyst Erin Yepis about the results of our 2024 Developer Survey, which polled more than 65,000 developers about the tools they use, the technologies they want to learn, their experiences at work, and much more. Erin highlights what the survey reveals about devs’ favorite programming languages (JavaScript, HTML, Python), the rise of Rust, the popularity of embedded technologies (Raspberry Pi, Arduino), developer sentiment around AI, and why tech debt tops the list of developer frustrations.\n\n![Image of Eira [Methodology](/2025/methodology/)\n\n# 2 Technology\n\nEach year we explore the tools and technologies developers are currently using and the ones they want to use.\n\nThis year, we included new questions about embedded technology tools and industry-sourced, community-vetted technology options.\n\n* [2.1. Most popular technologies →](#most-popular-technologies)\n* [2.2. Admired and Desired →](#admired-and-desired)\n* [2.3. Worked with vs. want to work with →](#worked-with-vs-want-to-work-with)\n\n## 2.1 [Most popular technologies](#most-popular-technologies)\n\n### [Programming, scripting, and markup languages](#1-programming-scripting-and-markup-languages)\n\nAfter more than a decade of steady growth, Python's adoption has accelerated significantly. It saw a 7 percentage point increase from 2024 to 2025; this speaks to its ability to be the go-to language for AI, data science, and back-end development.\n\nWhich **programming, scripting, and markup languages** have you done extensive development work in over the past year, and which do you want to work in over the next year? (If you both worked with the language and want to continue to do so, please check both boxes in that row.)\n\n[All Respondents](#most-popular-technologies-language-language)[Professional [Cloud development](#1-cloud-development)\n\nDocker has moved from a popular tool to a near-universal one. 31,569(64.4%)\n\n## 2.2 [Admired and Desired](#admired-and-desired)\n\nTo better gauge hype versus reality, we created a visualization that shows the distance between the proportion of respondents who want to use a technology (“desired”) and the proportion of users that have used the same technology in the past year and want to continue using it (“admired”).\n\n### [Programming, scripting, and markup languages](#2-programming-scripting-and-markup-languages)\n\nRust is yet again the most admired programming language (72%), followed by Gleam (70%), Elixir (66%) and Zig (64%). Gleam is a new addition to the list, and for good reason - developers like it!\n\nWhich **programming, scripting, and markup languages** have you done extensive development work in over the past year, and which do you want to work in over the next year?
